## Product acceptance

DiceBox Campaign Helper is successful when a user can create and manage a real local campaign project; author Scenes, Actors, Items, Activities, assets, and semantic geometry through accessible controls; converse with a Campaign Director that uses bounded project and knowledge-base context; review exact typed proposals; approve deterministic changes; compile versioned Foundry plans; execute only authorized target operations; and verify committed state through authoritative rereads and diagnostics.

The Campaign Director and Technical Builder use one managed ChatGPT/Codex account through Codex App Server by default. The API-key path remains an explicit, separately billed backup. Neither provider may bypass deterministic validation, approval, target authority, or reread.

## Current reality

## Mandatory change synchronization gate

Every accepted project change—whether requested by the user or proposed autonomously by Codex—must pass this ordered control-plane update before implementation work begins:

1. Record the accepted change and its impact in this roadmap and the active checklist.
2. Update the shared `Codex Workflow Ideas` Google Doc so the ChatGPT-side collaborator receives the same current direction.
3. Reconcile the change with the reproducible knowledge base, preserve conflicts and superseded guidance as provenance, then rebuild and verify the database.
4. Only after all three knowledge sources agree may source, package, runtime, fixture, or campaign implementation begin.

Read-only inspection, research, and emergency containment may occur before this gate completes, but they cannot silently become implementation. Post-implementation evidence returns through the normal reread and record phase. The existing database-first rule still requires consulting the current KB before planning; this gate defines the persistent update order for an accepted change.

- [x] Roadmap and active checklist updated for the synchronization gate.
- [x] Shared idea document updated and reread.
- [x] KB decision recorded, rebuilt, and verified.
- [x] Project contracts and machine checker enforce the synchronized order.

### Isolated workspace conflict-avoidance option — accepted 2026-08-26

The Foreman may place a bounded Worker assignment, risky build, or verification run in a separate Codex worktree or project-local disposable workspace when isolation materially reduces overlapping edits, build-artifact collisions, runtime-state interference, or evidence ambiguity. Isolation is an option, not a second authority plane: one Foreman still owns sequencing and integration; the canonical roadmap, active card, shared idea document, reproducible KB, source repository, and runtime remain authoritative in their existing domains. The isolated scope must start from an identified source state, exclude shared live-runtime control unless separately authorized, preserve user and foreign files, and return one compact receipt naming the source revision/state, changed paths, validation, provenance, blockers, and recovery disposition. Nothing is promoted automatically; the Foreman must reconcile and review the result at a clean boundary before integration. A separate workspace cannot bypass the accepted-change synchronization gate, grant Foundry authority, conceal conflicting edits, or create a competing project goal.

- [x] Roadmap and active checklist record the accepted isolation option.
- [x] Exact shared idea document records and rereads the same authority boundaries.
- [x] Reproducible KB records, rebuilds, and verifies the option under M224 / F-298 / D-258 / T-271.
- [x] Project contracts, consolidated workflow rules, and machine checker/tests enforce the option without making it mandatory for every task.

Both the foundation and repository `AGENTS.md` contracts, consolidated workflow rules, and active-slice checker preserve the optional worktree/disposable-workspace boundary. The checker rejects drift in exact identity, closure status, scope, authority, five prohibitions, deterministic check, and M224/M225 provenance; its focused test proves those failures. The deterministic KB summary fixture tracks 278 sources, 314 facts, 264 decisions, and 277 trials. Final `npm run check:full` passes 89 test files / 414 tests with TypeScript build, production bundle, workflow check, tooling verification, and KB integrity. This is local governance evidence only: it does not advance the seven-of-sixteen Stage 8 product count, create a workspace, authorize Foundry work, certify alpha, or complete the pending packaged detach-recovery proof.

Stages 0–6 are accepted **foundation proofs**, not proof that the complete product already exists. The current desktop still centers on a fixed semantic fixture, a narrow command model, a fixed proposal action, and one fixture-only Foundry operation. The checklist below begins at the first unresolved product gate rather than treating those proofs as finished user workflows.

Accepted vision-assisted Spatial Blueprint roadmap revision and Stage 8 continuation (2026-08-26): retain the Iggwilv Final Chamber as a legacy exact-stack fixture demonstrating that a known-size 1250 by 1250 renderer plus a 50-pixel grid can support useful visual object-location predictions and aligned Region proposals. Low-confidence, ambiguous-transform, cropped, aspect-ratio-mismatched, out-of-bounds, or stale-image results block promotion. This revision does not make map pixels geometry authority, certify the Iggwilv puzzle, add a seventeenth stage, or move future vision implementation ahead of the active ordered stages.

The capability is integrated across the existing roadmap rather than added as a competing stage: Stage 9 owns the Iggwilv fixture evidence, exact transform/hash receipts, installer/verifier parity, and authoritative Foundry reread; Stage 12 owns the editable vision-assisted proposal and Blueprint-promotion workflow; Stage 15 evaluates image providers on downstream landmark localization and grid-alignment fidelity as well as visual quality. - [x] Keep the Technical Builder a separate conditional job type with isolated scope and reviewed handoff. (M192 and M293-M295 preserve isolated changed-path/hash handoff with no implicit integration or runtime authority.)
- [x] Acceptance: a user can ask about the current project, receive a cited answer, request a typed edit proposal, and see no state change before approval. (M296 directly observes four persistent messages, citation, review-only proposal, and unchanged Scene revision/coordinates; M297 accepts the complete map.)

### 11. Generic proposal, approval, history, and recovery system

- [ ] Generalize the current fixture approval tray into inspectable command batches with dependencies, source/provider, hashes, authority, and risk.
- [ ] Add approve/reject/edit, stale invalidation, undo/redo, cancellation, timeout-as-unknown, reread-before-retry, and recovery receipts.
- [ ] Make human controls, Campaign Director proposals, Technical Builder patches, and Foundry plans share one review vocabulary without sharing authority.

### 12. Scene and asset authoring breadth

- [ ] Expand beyond rectangle translation to rooms, polygons, Walls, doors, Regions, Background Objects, native Foundry Tiles, lights, notes, grids, alignments, and relationships.
- [ ] Add layer visibility/locking, snapping, measurements, duplicate/delete, grouping, selection tools, property editing, validation overlays, and asset-library placement.
- [ ] Add vision-assisted Spatial Blueprint proposals for an approved known-size raster: detect semantic landmarks, record image-space centers/bounds and confidence, apply an explicit image-to-Scene/grid transform, snap and pad proposed Regions, and show an editable overlay before approval.
- [ ] Preserve Spatial Blueprint geometry authority and keep raster images as renderers: model output and pixels may propose geometry, but only GM-reviewed coordinates promoted through the semantic command path become authoritative.
- [ ] Acceptance: author a nontrivial multi-room Scene without editing source files or raw JSON, including one known-size map whose proposed object Regions are corrected or approved in an overlay and reread from the resulting Spatial Blueprint.

### 13. Desktop-controlled Foundry workflow

- [ ] Add exact target profiles and process/client readiness without browser-dependent status checks.
- [ ] Expose source/stage parity, module version, world/system versions, active-GM state, preview, approval, apply, authoritative reread, and recovery in the desktop.
- [ ] Keep fixture and campaign targets visibly distinct; require fresh authority immediately before every write.
- [ ] Acceptance: install and update one program-authored Scene from the desktop in the isolated fixture, then verify it without using developer macros or manual console work.

### 14. Campaign content model and compiler

- [ ] Enable Actors, Items, Activities, Journals, encounters, puzzles, stable runtime bindings, and module-owned behavior plans in the desktop.
- [ ] Add schema-aware forms, references, validation, compendium lookup, and migration support.
- [ ] Compile full campaign packages without granting runtime authority or replacing foreign state.
- [ ] Acceptance: build a small campaign slice with one Scene, two Actors, Items/Activities, and a puzzle plan, then compile and inspect a deterministic package.

### 15. Image providers

- [ ] Implement the provider contract and Structural Render Package after the authoring and approval spine can consume candidates correctly.
- [ ] Run the same corpus through GPT Image and selected challengers; record topology, edit fidelity, forbidden-object leakage, landmark localization, grid-alignment fidelity, latency, cost, hashes, and versions.

- [ ] Keep image credentials provider-specific and separate from the Codex subscription/API-key choice.
- [ ] Promote a default only after the rubric passes; otherwise use deterministic composition.
- [ ] Acceptance: a candidate is reproducible, reviewable, and rejectable without changing Scene geometry, and at least one benchmark demonstrates a bounded editable object-location overlay whose accepted coordinates can be promoted separately into the Spatial Blueprint.

### 16. Product hardening and release

- [x] **First step — research:** Look online for UI interfaces that are simplified for the learning impaired. Research completed 2026-08-28 using W3C Consistent Navigation and COGA guidance, UK Government cognitive-accessibility guidance, and Section 508 cognitive-disability guidance. The resulting requirements are consistent placement/order, plain-language labels, keyboard-operable disclosure controls, visible orientation and feedback, manageable sections, and adaptable text presentation.
- [ ] Test Windows/macOS/Linux WebViews and execute the documented Electron fallback review if needed.
- [ ] Complete accessibility, scaling, large-project, offline, provider-failure, security, backup, update, and privacy testing.
- [ ] Add signed installers, update channels, diagnostics export, onboarding, and user documentation.
- [ ] Run end-to-end acceptance with a real program-authored project and exact isolated Foundry proof.

#### Final-phase account and navigation UI checklist — ordered, research first

- [x] Look online for UI interfaces that are simplified for the learning impaired.
- [ ] Convert the research into reviewed acceptance criteria and a small-screen/keyboard/screen-reader interaction map.
- [ ] Design a persistent Settings area for non-project settings; keep API credentials out of browser/project artifacts and use the existing OS credential boundary.
- [ ] Add a bottom-left ChatGPT account button with an official, supported account-connection flow; do not invent a password or OAuth protocol, and keep API-key backup separate.
- [ ] Place the Campaign Director / ChatGPT conversation on the left side of the workspace in its own vertically split pane. Preserve predictable order, independently operable keyboard focus, visible resize/state feedback, and a usable narrow-window layout; do not combine this with an unsupported account-login flow.
- [ ] Reorganize the right-side scroll surface into a small number of clearly labeled, keyboard-operable disclosure groups with stable order and visible current location.
- [ ] Validate accessible names, focus order, error feedback, text scaling/spacing, reduced motion, and persistence/recovery before Stage 16 acceptance.

The first item is a completed `research` gate, not a product acceptance claim. The settings, account-connection, and navigation items remain pending until their own synchronized implementation bundles are selected in the ordered roadmap.

Research sources: [W3C Consistent Navigation](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG21/Understanding/consistent-navigation), [W3C COGA design guide](https://www.w3.org/TR/coga-usable/design_guide.html), [UK Government cognitive accessibility guidelines](https://deliver-good-services.education.gov.uk/guides/guidelines/cognitive-accessibility-guidelines), [Section 508 cognitive-disability guidance](https://www.section508.gov/design/digital-content-users-with-cognitive-disabilities/), and [OpenAI API authentication](https://developers.openai.com/api/reference/overview).

## Research and recovery rule

- On the second failure of the same class, stop retries and repair proliferation.
- Query the KB first. If it lacks a proven answer, research authoritative technical documentation and directly relevant implementations/community reports.
- Reduce the failure to a bounded reproduction, implement the narrowest fix, validate locally, then attempt one new live run.
- Record a workflow instruction only after it works and is authoritatively reread; keep unsuccessful research as provisional evidence.

## Work limits

- Keep exactly one active acceptance stage. At most one build, one live, and one research/recovery item may be active.
- Do not resume Tram or another campaign slice until explicitly reprioritized after the product spine can author and compile it.
- A source test, rendered canvas, provider response, successful transport, or API existence is not product acceptance.
- API-key fallback is always opt-in. Never switch from subscription access to usage-based billing automatically.
